| **[Sharp Regret Bounds and a Task-Covariance Correction for Spectral Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.15825v3)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Spectral contrastive learning keeps the $k$ directions along which two views of the data depend most strongly, while downstream tasks may need other directions. Existing guarantees for spectral features assume that the distribution of tasks has no preferred direction. We show that this isotropy condition is the wrong criterion. With linear probing, the task distribution affects the average probing gain only through the matrix $BΛB^\top$, where $B$ records the dependence between views and $Λ$ is the task covariance: optimal features span its top-$k$ eigenspace, whereas spectral features span that of $BB^\top$. Transfer therefore depends on alignment, and strongly uneven task preferences cost nothing when they favour the spectral directions. Otherwise the regret of spectral features is at most linear in the anisotropy of $Λ$. No representation chosen without task information improves this rate when the leading spectrum is flat, and a spectral gap makes it quadratic. With labelled tasks, the same matrix gives a correction: Fisher discriminant directions for a frozen encoder, or a one-term change to the spectral contrastive loss. We bound the regret of the correction by the error in estimating $BΛB^\top$, and compare this error with the misalignment of spectral features to decide when to correct. The analysis extends to finite samples, continuous variables and noisy views. Correction reduces synthetic held-out regret from $0.858$ to $0.003$ with $2000$ labelled tasks, and the decision rule gives the lowest regret among the tested rules at every bank size. On CIFAR-100, spectral features of frozen encoders keep most of the task signal on unseen classes, and where a bank of other classes would lower accuracy, the rule keeps them in every case.</p></details> | <details><summary>45 pa...</summary><p>45 pages, 4 figures, 15 tables. Feedback and comments are welcome!</p></details> |
| **[PACE-ECG: Patient-Aware Contrastive ECG Pretraining; Leveraging Longitudinal Structure for Transferable Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12307v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Foundation models for physiological signals are commonly evaluated on in-domain interpretation tasks, but their ability to transfer to longitudinal clinical prognosis across healthcare systems remains uncertain. | **[RAIL: Representation-Aligned Imitation Learning for Student-Compatible Teacher Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12251v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reinforcement learning (RL) from raw sensory inputs such as images or onboard sensors can be challenging due to high-dimensional observations and sparse rewards. However, when the teacher relies on information unavailable to the student, exact imitation may be impossible, creating an irreducible imitation gap. Existing approaches typically mitigate this mismatch through careful reward shaping or additional reinforcement learning on the student policy after the teacher has been trained. Instead, we introduce Representation-Aligned Imitation Learning (RAIL), which addresses the mismatch during teacher training itself. RAIL learns a latent representation shared across teacher and student observations using contrastive learning, and trains the teacher policy directly in this space. Because the teacher policy is constrained to operate on this shared space, it learns behaviors that are reproducible from student observations. This substantially reduces the imitation gap while preserving task performance. Across multiple environments, RAIL outperforms strong baselines without reward modification or post-hoc student fine-tuning, and surpasses direct reinforcement learning from raw observations. Although contrastive learning offers a promising approach, applying it to multi-label classification presents unique challenges, particularly in managing label interactions and data structure. In this paper, we conduct an in-depth study of contrastive learning loss for multi-label classification across diverse settings. On 3DO and Widar3.0 it is 3.77 and 13.11 pp ahead of the strongest of 21 baselines, which uses replay; on WiMANS it matches the strongest replay baseline in accuracy while reducing forgetting from 2.78 to 0.00 pp. Rank-32 covariance factors shrink the moment memory to 6.4% at a cost of at most 0.49 pp.</p></details> | <details><summary>13 pa...</summary><p>13 pages, 6 figures, 10 tables; supplementary material: 13 pages. This work has been submitted to the IEEE for possible publication. Copyright may be transferred without notice, after which this version may no longer be accessible. Code: https://github.com/Newsteinwell/WiMoT</p></details> |
| **[ProAlign: Progressive Positional and Prototype-Guided Alignment for Aerial-Ground Person Re-Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04980v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Aerial-Ground Person Re-Identification (AG-ReID) aims to match pedestrian images captured by unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) and ground-based surveillance cameras. The task remains highly challenging due to severe viewpoint discrepancies, frequent occlusions, and substantial domain gaps between aerial and ground imagery. Beyond these observable factors, existing methods often overlook two fundamental structural issues: cumulative positional drift during hierarchical feature extraction and semantic inconsistency across cross-view feature distributions. To address these challenges, we propose ProAlign, a Progressive Positional and Prototype-Guided Alignment Network for AG-ReID. ProAlign comprises two key components: a Layer-wise Progressive Positional Embedding (LPPE) module and a View-Aware Prototype Contrastive Learning (PCL) module. LPPE performs adaptive positional calibration throughout transformer layers by employing conditional positional encoding in shallow layers to mitigate local spatial distortions, while introducing learnable static positional embeddings in deeper layers to reinforce global semantic priors. Meanwhile, PCL maintains view-specific aerial and ground prototypes for each identity and enforces cross-view semantic consistency via a dual-prototype contrastive objective. Extensive experiments on CARGO and two real-world AG-ReID benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of ProAlign, achieving state-of-the-art overall retrieval performance on CARGO with improvements of 1.60% in Rank-1 accuracy and 5.01% in mAP over the strongest competing methods.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted by NeurIPS 2026</p></details> |
| **[PROSE: Preservation-Constrained Selective Editing for Guilty Bias in Legal Judgment Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04921v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Legal judgment prediction models may retain a guilty verdict after a legally decisive condition has been altered such that the specified charge is no longer established. We construct CAIL-MC, a legally reviewed dataset of 11,478 matched pairs covering 261 Chinese criminal charges. Held-out activation interventions show that contrasts learned from correctly resolved pairs transfer to false-guilty cases, but target-reaching interventions can also damage matched supported-guilty decisions. Motivated by this observation, we introduce PROSE, which reconstructs case-specific targets from a training-derived residual geometry, aligns them with a local margin-descent signal, attenuates supported-guilty-sensitive components, and realizes bounded targets through a preservation-regularized low-rank parameter update. | **[PAT: A Personality-Driven Augmentation and Transfer Learning Framework for Depression Detection on Social Media](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.15044v2)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Depression is a prevalent mental disorder that affects millions worldwide, with early detection crucial for effective intervention. To address these limita?tions, we propose PAT, a Personality-driven Augmentation and Transfer learning frame?work. In the first phase, PAT optimizes a post?level encoder via a fine-grained contrastive learning objective, utilizing LLM-based data augmentation driven by classic personality the?ories to alleviate data scarcity. In the second phase, the trained post-level scorer is deployed for high-quality post filtering, while its en?coder parameters are seamlessly transferred for user-level tasks. Extensive experiments demon?strate that PAT significantly outperforms exist?ing methods. Furthermore, PAT delivers multi?granular interpretability by tracing post-level mental-state trajectories and highlighting key symptoms, thereby offering reliable diagnostic evidence for clinical practice.</p></details> |  |
| **[Learning to Rank Explanations for Recommendation with Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04775v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Recommender systems are ubiquitous on the Web, making understandable explanations essential for users. However, existing statement ranking methods largely rely on collaborative representations, while semantic enhancements often fail to outperform simple user-history popularity baselines. Moreover, directly adapting language models for personalized statement ranking remains largely unexplored. In this work, we introduce LMStaR (Language Models for Statement Ranking), a fully text-based framework that formulates personalized explanation ranking as dense retrieval. A query encoder represents the user's interaction history and target-item metadata, while a statement encoder maps candidate explanations into a shared embedding space. Both encoders are jointly adapted through multi-positive contrastive learning to learn personalized relevance. Experiments on Amazon Beauty, Amazon Toys, and Yelp show that LMStaR achieves the highest NDCG@1 across all three domains, outperforming CLER by 1.06--8.25 points (+11.8--65.9%). It also leads top-five ranking on both Amazon datasets, although CLER remains stronger on Yelp. Counterfactual analyses further reveal the importance of user-specific histories. These findings highlight the potential of task-adapted language models for personalized explanation ranking.</p></details> | <details><summary>12 pa...</summary><p>12 pages, 5 figures, 7 tables</p></details> |
| **[Uncertainty-Aware Prototype Learning for Open-Set Test-Time Adaptation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04689v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Open-set test-time adaptation (TTA) aims to adapt source-trained models to unlabeled test streams where covariate-shifted in-distribution (csID) samples and covariate-shifted out-of-distribution (csOOD) samples may coexist. Consequently, csOOD samples may be mistakenly included in confidence-enhancing adaptation, producing harmful gradients that degrade known-class performance. To address this, we propose Uncertainty-Aware Prototype Learning for Open-Set Test-Time Adaptation (UAPA), a plug-and-play method for reliable open-set adaptation. Specifically, we first introduce Uncertainty-Driven ID/OOD Separation, which combines a classifier-prototype score with a mini-batch-normalized evidence-derived uncertainty score to distinguish csID from csOOD samples. We then propose a Dynamic Prototype Refinement strategy that periodically refines class-specific target prototypes using buffered csID features to progressively follow the estimated class directions. Lastly, we introduce Contrastive Prototype Entropy Adaptation, which applies entropy minimization to csID samples and entropy maximization to csOOD samples, while prototype-level contrastive learning aligns csID features with their corresponding target prototypes and reduces the maximum similarity between csOOD features and known-class prototypes. These results suggest that automated polyp matching has the potential to reduce polyp over-counting, improve patient risk stratification, and reduce unnecessary follow-up colonoscopies.</p></details> |  |

| **[Domain-Adaptive Deep Joint Source-Channel Coding for Image Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.28907v2)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Deep joint source--channel coding (Deep JSCC) can suffer from domain shift between training and deployment data. We further proposed an information objective to reduce domain information while preserving class information. The former uses all target samples to reduce class-conditional domain differences, while the latter selects high-confidence target samples to obtain class-discriminative received representations. | **[Reliability-Role Collaborative Learning for Semi-Supervised Cardiac Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04219v1)** | 2026-11-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Semi-supervised cardiac image segmentation aims to exploit abundant unlabeled images together with limited annotations, but the reliability of pseudo-label supervision is highly uneven across spatial regions. However, existing methods typically impose uniform supervision on all unlabeled pixels, overlooking reliability differences and limiting discriminative learning in challenging areas. To address this issue, we propose R2CL (Reliability-Role Collaborative Learning), a framework for semi-supervised cardiac image segmentation. R2CL models confidence relationships between two networks to assign roles to unlabeled regions: mutually uncertain regions are used for entropy regularization, confidence-discrepant regions for directional knowledge transfer, and mutually confident regions as reliable supervision anchors. Reliability-Role Mixed Cross Learning (RR-MCL) implements these roles through region-mixed training, directional cross-network supervision, and entropy regularization over mutually uncertain regions. Reliability-Transition Prototype Contrastive Learning (RT-PCL) constructs query features from foreground-adjacent reliability-transition regions and learns network-specific class prototypes from mutually confident regions, improving feature discrimination around ambiguous boundaries. High-confidence Context Refinement (HCR) exploits reliable interior pixels and trusted dense blocks within mutually confident regions to complement stable contextual supervision. R2CL achieves stable improvements in region-overlap and boundary-related metrics across multiple datasets, with more evident advantages on the foreground-sparse MVAA-CT dataset.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted at IEEE BIBM 2026. 7 pages, 4 figures</p></details> |
| **[Decision Transformer with Task-Aware Representations for Offline Meta-Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.04010v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>To enable effective generalization in offline meta-RL, it is essential to accurately infer the underlying task through latent task representations, which provide a compact and transferable summary of task-specific characteristics for guiding policy generation. The learned task representations are used as conditioning signals for a DT-based policy, enabling task-aware meta-policy learning. To further support generalization in both zero-shot and few-shot settings, we design a language-grounded, self-adaptive strategy that enables effective and reliable adaptation to unseen tasks. Notably, in CoTA-DT, the pretrained LLM serves as a shared backbone for language encoding, world modeling, Q-function estimation, and policy learning. Extensive experiments across diverse offline meta-RL benchmarks demonstrate that CoTA-DT consistently outperforms prior methods, particularly in challenging zero-shot generalization scenarios.</p></details> |  |
| **[Contrastive Adapter Training and Consensus Knowledge Distillation for Multi-Source-Free Domain Adaptation in Skin Cancer Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03528v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Skin cancer diagnosis, particularly differentiating melanoma from benign nevi, is challenging due to the visual similarity between lesions. Although convolutional neural networks (CNNs) and vision transformers (ViTs) have shown promising performance, their effectiveness often deteriorates on data from heterogeneous clinical sources. Conventional domain adaptation methods address domain shift but require access to source data during adaptation, which is often infeasible due to privacy regulations. Multi-source-free unsupervised domain adaptation (MSFDA) addresses this limitation by leveraging multiple labeled source domains to generalize to an unlabeled target domain without requiring access to source data. However, existing MSFDA methods rely on full backbone fine-tuning, leading to catastrophic forgetting and overfitting on small clinical datasets, and address domain shift at the aggregation stage without establishing a shared domain-invariant feature space. Furthermore, their reliance on hard pseudo-labels or confidence-weighted aggregation introduces noisy supervision signals under domain shift. To address these limitations, we propose CAT-CKD, consisting of two components: (1) contrastive adapter training (CAT), which trains lightweight ConvPass adapters within a frozen ViT backbone using supervised contrastive learning (SCL) to establish a shared domain-invariant feature space before source-specific model training, and (2) consensus knowledge distillation (CKD), which aggregates logits from multiple source models into a consensus supervisory signal and adapts a student model on unlabeled target data using KL divergence. Experiments on five publicly available skin lesion datasets show that CAT-CKD achieves an average AUROC of 86.1%, outperforming existing MSFDA methods while requiring only 4.3M trainable parameters. The code is available at https://github.com/A-Abedi/CAT_CKD.</p></details> | <details><summary>Publi...</summary><p>Published in Artificial Intelligence in Medicine, Vol. 180, 103465 (2026). DOI: https://doi.org/10.1016/j.artmed.2026.103465. Available at: https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S093336572600117X and Code is available at: https://github.com/A-Abedi/CAT_CKD</p></details> |
| **[SQLoom: Set-Contrastive Learning of Syntactic, Semantic, and Logical Dependencies for Text-to-SQL](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03298v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Text-to-SQL translates natural language questions into SQL queries, enabling database access without writing SQL. Fine-tuning open-weight large language models (LLMs) is cost-effective, but existing methods treat SQL as ordinary text and optimize token-level likelihood without explicitly modeling its syntactic, semantic, and logical requirements. Thus, a single incorrect alias or comparison operator can invalidate a query while contributing little to training loss. We propose SQLoom, a constraint-aware fine-tuning framework with supervision at three levels: syntactic validity under SQL grammar and database schema, semantic alignment between query decisions and the question, and logical consistency across dependent clause decisions. SQLoom extends supervised fine-tuning with a unified set-contrastive objective favoring sets of correct outputs over minimally different incorrect ones at each level. LLM agents propose this supervision, which a SQL parser and execution on counterexample databases verify. We also release Spider-Refined, a training dataset correcting 378 flawed Spider annotations and enriching database schemas. Experiments on Spider, BIRD, and three perturbed Spider variants show that SQLoom improves execution accuracy for Qwen3-1.7B from 62.9% zero-shot to 88.7% on Spider development, reaching 89.2% on Spider test and 73.4% on BIRD development. On question perturbations, SQLoom loses at most 0.6 accuracy points versus up to 12.1 for agent-based methods. These results demonstrate the effectiveness of set-contrastive supervision over token-level training.</p></details> |  |
| **[Robust Scene Transfer for PointGoal Navigation via Privileged Sensor Guided Contrastive Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.05506v4)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>We propose a sensor-guided adaptive contrastive learning framework for visual representation learning in PointGoal navigation. Extensive experiments in high-fidelity simulation demonstrate that our approach significantly improves policy-level scene transfer across diverse indoor and outdoor environments. At deployment, the agent relies only on monocular RGB observations together with standard task-related inputs such as goal position and proprioceptive signals, without access to LiDAR or other privileged sensors. Our method outperforms large pretrained vision models and standard contrastive baselines under severe appearance and semantic shifts. We also release a multimodal dataset to support future research on privileged-guided visual representation learning for navigation. The code is available at:</p></details> | <details><summary>8 pag...</summary><p>8 pages, Accepted to IEEE Robotics and Automation Letters (RA-L)</p></details> |
| **[Unlocking the Potential of Contrastive Regularization for Diffusion Transformer Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02921v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Representation regularization has emerged as an effective way to improve diffusion transformer training. Our analysis reveals that the objective is easily dominated by a shortcut: near-orthogonal noise components in diffusion inputs provide a bypass for enlarging negative-pair distances while weakening positive-pair alignment, especially at high noise levels. To unlock the benefit of contrastive regularization, we propose Clean Representation-ANchored Contrastive (CRAN) regularization. Specifically, CRAN anchors contrastive dispersion with clean-sample representation distances, transferring clean relational semantic structure to noisy diffusion representations to suppress the noise shortcut. | **[MSAlign: Aligning Molecule and Mass Spectra Representations for Metabolite Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.19752v3)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Accurately identifying metabolites i.e. small molecules from mass spectrometry data remains a core challenge in metabolomics, with broad applications in drug discovery, environmental analysis, and clinical research. | **[Stage-aware Multimodal Brain Graph Contrastive Learning for Neurodegenerative Disorder Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02771v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Neurodegenerative disorders are characterized by their progressive nature, with distinct stages that require precise identification and tracking of progression trajectories for accurate diagnosis. | **[Geometry-Constrained Dynamic Hypergraph Convolutional Network with Contrastive Score Refinement for Skeleton-Based Action Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02685v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Skeleton-based action recognition is a pivotal task in computer vision; however, conventional Graph Convolutional Networks (GCNs) are often constrained by pairwise joint modeling, which fails to capture the intricate high-order interactions inherent in human movement. To this end, we propose DyGCN, a Geometry-Constrained Dynamic Hypergraph Convolutional Network for skeleton-based action recognition. At the core of DyGCN is the Dynamic Hypergraph Construction Network with Contrastive Score Refinement (DyGCT) block, which comprises two novel components: the Geometry-Constrained Dynamic Hypergraph Construction (GeoHC) module and the Contrastive Score Refinement (CSR) module. Specifically, GeoHC dynamically constructs action-specific hyperedges by leveraging geometry-constrained second-order compatibility, transcending the limitations of static hyperedge structures. Furthermore, CSR explicitly refines the pairwise compatibility scores during hypergraph construction through prototype-based contrastive learning, fostering more discriminative action-specific hyperedges, with zero inference-time overhead as CSR operates only during training. Extensive experiments on NTU RGB+D, NTU RGB+D 120, and Northwestern-UCLA validate the effectiveness of DyGCN, achieving state-of-the-art performance on widely used benchmarks. | **[VLM2Rec: Resolving Modality Collapse in Vision-Language Models for Sequential Recommendation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.17450v3)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Sequential Recommendation (SR) learns sequence-level Collaborative Filtering (CF) from interactions, but ID-based models struggle with sparse data and cold-start items. In contrast, item titles and images can reveal preference continuity, such as a recurring color or style. Yet existing approaches miss visual continuity: small encoders take a single item as input, and Large Language Models (LLMs) read only titles. We therefore shift our focus to Large Vision-Language Models (VLMs), which can read a user's entire image sequence, not just its titles. However, VLMs inherently suffer from modality collapse: they over-rely on text and, as a result, overlook visual continuity for recommendation. We find that standard contrastive Supervised Fine-Tuning (SFT), essential for adapting VLMs to SR, further amplifies this collapse: updates follow the text gradient, visual discrimination weakens, and adding image embeddings to the text embeddings consistently lowers accuracy. We propose VLM2Rec with two objectives: Weak-modality Penalized Contrastive Learning strengthens each user's weaker modality, and Cross-modal Relational Topology Regularization aligns each user's relative similarity structure over the same items across the two modality spaces. Experiments show that VLM2Rec mitigates the collapse and consistently improves accuracy and robustness.</p></details> |  |
| **[ReplayFactor: Replay-Aware Dual-Attribute Learning for Practical Speech Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02628v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Advances in synthesis techniques have made deepfake speech increasingly realistic, amplifying voice security threats, including unauthorized voice cloning and impersonation. Submitted to ICASSP 2027</p></details> |
| **[CaRe-KT: Continuity-Aware Retrieval-Augmented Reasoning for Knowledge Tracing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.03744v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Knowledge tracing (KT) estimates learners' evolving knowledge states from interaction histories to support adaptive learning. Retrieval-augmented KT improves these estimates using cross-learner behavioral evidence, but existing approaches typically separate evidence retrieval from downstream prediction. This creates a retrieval-inference alignment gap because evidence relevance varies with the learner's evolving state and prediction context. We propose CaRe-KT, a query-adaptive framework that maintains relevance continuity between evidence acquisition and inference without equating retrieval relevance with predictive utility. CaRe-KT introduces an Evidence-Graph Cache (EGC) that combines persistent question-level relational compatibility with interaction-level temporal validity to construct query-specific evidence subgraphs. Historical learning trajectories retrieved through these subgraphs are encoded alongside the target learner's history in a shared representation space using outcome-supervised contrastive learning. An attention mechanism combines acquisition relevance with learned query-evidence compatibility, allowing relevance to inform, rather than determine, each trajectory's predictive influence. Experiments across five benchmarks show that CaRe-KT outperforms strong KT baselines and remains robust under sparse-history and cold-start conditions. These findings support relevance continuity as a design principle for retrieval-augmented knowledge tracing.</p></details> | <details><summary>13 pa...</summary><p>13 pages, 4 figures, 4 tables</p></details> |
| **[Multi-Teacher Reasoning Distillation for LLM Recommendation with Self-Exploration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02457v1)** | 2026-11-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reasoning-augmented recommender systems (RecSys) enhance user preference modeling by generating reasoning trajectories over historical interactions before making recommendations. | **[Peripheral prototypical contrastive loss for supervised medical image segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.02305v1)** | 2026-11-01 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Robust segmentation of biomedical images remains challenging primarily due to ill-defined object boundaries. Extensive cross-subject experiments on SEED, SEED-IV, SEED-V, and FACED show that DP-STCL consistently outperforms the compared methods, demonstrating its effectiveness in improving cross-subject EEG emotion recognition.</p></details> |  |
| **[TransECG-SSL: Hybrid Self-Supervised Representation Learning for ECG Arrhythmia Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35378v1)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Cross-dataset generalisation remains a major challenge for automated electrocardiogram (ECG) arrhythmia classification because of differences in patient populations, acquisition protocols, annotation practices, and class distributions. | **[AutoPRL: Automated Path Representation Learning via a Learnable View Generator](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.35032v1)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Self-supervised learning has emerged as a powerful paradigm for Path Representation Learning (PRL) in road networks. We further propose the Class Desensitization Loss by employing contrastive learning to correct edge biases caused by data imbalance. Moreover, we dynamically fuse the weight parameters of the old and new models to achieve a better trade-off between the biased and unbiased model weights. Accepted at NeurIPS 2026</p></details> |
| **[TimeWorld: Towards Action-Conditioned Multimodal World Models for Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34797v1)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Time series forecasting supports decision-making in web applications such as online retail and network management, where planned actions can reshape future dynamics. Specifically, the Prototype-Guided Latent Modulation (PLM) module grounds textual actions in temporal dynamics by composing representative response prototypes to modulate future representations based on the action and historical patterns. | **[On the Value of Cross-Modal Misalignment in Multimodal Representation Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.10143v9)** | 2026-10-25 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal representation learning, exemplified by multimodal contrastive learning (MMCL) using image-text pairs, aims to learn powerful representations by aligning cues across modalities. There are two distinct viewpoints on how to address this issue: one suggests mitigating the misalignment, and the other leveraging it. We seek here to reconcile these seemingly opposing perspectives and to provide a practical guide for practitioners. Using latent variable models, we thus formalize cross-modal misalignment by introducing two specific mechanisms: \emph{selection bias}, where some semantic variables are absent in the text, and \emph{perturbation bias}, where semantic variables are altered---both leading to misalignment in data pairs. Our theoretical analysis shows that, under suitable assumptions, population global minimizers of an alignment--entropy surrogate yield representations that depend only on the semantic variables unaffected by selection and perturbation biases and locally identify this subset almost surely. Extensive empirical studies on synthetic data and real image-text datasets further examine how cross-modal misalignment shapes semantic predictability and downstream performance, shedding light on its nuanced impact on multimodal representation learning.</p></details> | <details><summary>v9: C...</summary><p>v9: Corrected global to local block-identifiability (a.s.); see Appx. D.2</p></details> |
| **[PIT-GCL: Protein Interaction using Topological Graph Contrastive Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.04850v3)** | 2026-10-25 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Protein binding prediction is central to target identification, therapeutic binder design, and large scale screening, yet remains challenging because binding depends on sequence, three dimensional geometry, and global structural organization. | **[LoCA: Enabling Multi-Modality in Geospatial Location Encoders](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32894v1)** | 2026-10-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Location encoders map geographic coordinates to high-dimensional descriptors through contrastive alignment with paired Earth Observation imagery. We prove that this construction is structurally limited: any Lipschitz location encoder produces an output of intrinsic dimension at most 2, regardless of architecture or bandwidth, which forces the contrastive loss to strictly exceed its asymptotic optimum. Empirically, joint training also collapses the encoder's embedding covariance to low rank. Although dimension-limited, current location encoders sidestep this spectral collapse by training against a frozen image encoder, which we reinterpret as a covariance regularizer that supplies a high-rank target for the location encoder to fold onto. However, extending this approach to multimodal inputs is challenging, as location encoders should simultaneously map a single location to multiple, potentially incompatible, descriptors. We identify two requirements a regularizer must satisfy for location encoding in multimodal regimes: co-alignment across modalities, since adapters alone cannot align independently pretrained encoders; and high effective rank, since the location encoder inherits its target's spectrum. We propose LoCA (Location Contrastive Alignment), a two-stage pipeline that satisfies both. | **[Internal Discrepancy for Contrastive Learning: Generalization Bounds and Downstream Behaviors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.32729v1)** | 2026-10-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Contrastive learning has emerged as a popular paradigm for training representation models. Finally, we investigate the optimality of these incurred supervised losses, proving several intriguing properties.</p></details> |  |

| **[NS-Net: Decoupling CLIP Semantic Information through NULL-Space for Generalizable AI-Generated Image Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2508.01248v5)** | 2026-10-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The rapid progress of generative models, such as GANs and diffusion models, has facilitated the creation of highly realistic images, raising growing concerns over their misuse in security-sensitive domains. | **[ProCLIP: Progressive Vision-Language Alignment via LLM-based Embedder](https://arxiv.org/abs/2510.18795v5)** | 2026-10-23 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>The original CLIP text encoder is limited by a maximum input length of 77 tokens, which hampers its ability to effectively process long texts and perform fine-grained semantic understanding. ## Multimodal Fusion
| **Title** | **Date** | **Abstract** | **Comment** |

| **[Interpretable Context-Aware Multi-Label Retinal Classification with Dual-Hub Heterogeneous Graph Neural Network](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.09309v2)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multi-label retinal screening requires capturing co-occurring pathologies and clinical context, yet standard multimodal fusion remains opaque. We propose a dual-hub heterogeneous graph neural network (GNN) that couples spatial fundus features and structured clinical covariates through cross-modal message passing while providing inherent interpretability. Rather than relying on post-hoc saliency approximations, our architecture exposes multi-level decision pathways during forward aggregation: spatially biased self-attention captures lesion-level patch interactions, while modality-specific graph attention coefficients explicitly quantify the contribution of visual regions and clinical risk factors to each disease node. Evaluated on the BRSET dataset (16,266 photographs from 8,524 patients), the proposed model achieves 76.1% Macro-AUPRC across seven conditions, matching or exceeding image-only (75.9%) and ResNet-50+MLP fusion (75.9%) baselines, with largest contextual gains in macular edema (+3.5 percentage points, pp) and diabetic retinopathy (+2.1 pp). Codebase is available at: https://github.com/proshanto-c/ca-mlc-refactor.</p></details> |  |
| **[Leveraging Complementary Event Frame and Voxel Representations for RGB-Guided Multimodal Pre-training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.12040v1)** | 2026-11-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>In this paper, we propose CM3AE 2.0, a unified RGB Frame and Event-Voxel/-Frame pre-training framework. By jointly learning multimodal spatiotemporal representations and exploiting latent cross-modal dependencies through cross-modal generation, the framework enhances multimodal information interaction and collaborative modeling capabilities. Specifically, we first propose an Event-aware Spatiotemporal Masked Reconstruction (ESMR) strategy to learn unified multimodal spatiotemporal representations. It leverages event responses to guide masking toward dynamically salient regions. Through spatiotemporal masked reconstruction, it learns spatial structures and their temporal variations from continuous RGB-Event observations. In addition, it incorporates multimodal fusion reconstruction and cross-modal contrastive learning to facilitate the modeling of cross-modal complementary information and the learning of shared representations. Building upon this, we further propose a Hierarchical Cross-modal Generation (HCG) strategy to learn latent cross-modal dependencies through visual and language generation. At the visual level, Event-conditioned RGB generation is performed using a pre-trained diffusion model to capture visual associations between the two modalities. At the language level, video-level text description generation based on joint RGB-Event representations establishes connections between multimodal visual features and linguistic semantics, further enriching the learning of cross-modal dependencies. | **[Conditional Diffusion-Based Proxy Representation Recovery for BCVA Prediction with Missing SLO Modality](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.11326v1)** | 2026-11-08 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Preoperative prediction of postoperative best-corrected visual acuity (BCVA) can support prognosis assessment and individualized decision-making in cataract surgery. Instead of reconstructing raw SLO images, the proposed method formulates SLO missingness as proxy representation recovery in the latent feature space. Specifically, OCT image features and clinical text features are first fused to construct an OCT-text conditional representation, which guides the reverse denoising process of the diffusion model to estimate sample-specific SLO proxy representations. The proposed method achieved an accuracy of 0.9714 under the criterion of an absolute prediction error within 0.1 LogMAR and a mean absolute error of 0.0423, outperforming representative postoperative visual acuity prediction and missing-modality learning methods. These results indicate that representation-level proxy recovery can alleviate the impact of SLO modality missingness on postoperative BCVA prediction.</p></details> | <details><summary>8 pag...</summary><p>8 pages, 3 figures, 3 tables. Accepted at IEEE BIBM 2026</p></details> |

The two cross-attention directions exhibit distinct performance characteristics, demonstrating that reversing the query and source modalities does not produce interchangeable representations. Ablation experiments further show that bidirectional interaction, adaptive gating, and token guidance provide complementary benefits. The proposed model achieves 96.05% accuracy, 96.10% F1-score, 98.37% AUC, 95.60% Sens@95Spec, and 96.51% Spec@95Sens, yielding the highest mean performance across all evaluated metrics. | **[Task-Centric Multimodal Learning for Cognitive Impairment Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.20906v2)** | 2026-10-27 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Dementia affects millions of older adults worldwide, yet early detection remains difficult outside clinical settings. We pursued three objectives: (1) evaluate which of the three Alexa tasks provides the most informative cognitive signals, (2) compare the effectiveness of unimodal models, and (3) assess the benefits of multimodal fusion models. Task-level analysis shows that the video model achieves its best performance on Command-Reading, with an accuracy of 0.65 (AUC 0.69), while the audio model performs best on Question-Answering, reaching an accuracy of 0.67 (AUC 0.73). In contrast, the multimodal model demonstrates consistently stable performance across tasks, achieving an accuracy of 0.76 (AUC 0.75) on Command-Reading and 0.76 (AUC 0.73) on Question-Answering. | **[TimeWorld: Towards Action-Conditioned Multimodal World Models for Time Series Forecasting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.34797v1)** | 2026-10-26 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Time series forecasting supports decision-making in web applications such as online retail and network management, where planned actions can reshape future dynamics. Our evaluation reveals that conventional multimodal forecasters underperform history-only time series foundation models (TSFMs) despite dataset-specific training, whereas simple text-prefix adaptations improve forecasting across all evaluated TSFM backbones. These findings suggest that pretrained TSFMs encode implicit world knowledge of temporal dynamics, offering a strong foundation for action-conditioned modeling beyond conventional multimodal fusion. Building on this insight, we propose TimeWorld, a plug-in framework that adapts pretrained TSFMs through explicit action-response modeling. | **[GeoMER: A Joint Euclidean-Hyperbolic Geometry Framework for Multimodal Emotion Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.18587v1)** | 2026-10-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal Emotion Recognition (MER) aims to understand human emotions by integrating information from multiple modalities such as speech and text. Psychological studies suggest that emotions exhibit latent hierarchical structured relationships in their semantic representations. However, most MER approaches rely on Euclidean representations, which capture fine-grained cross-modal interactions but do not explicitly provide a geometric prior for organizing hierarchical emotional structures. To mitigate this gap, we propose GeoMER, a joint Euclidean-Hyperbolic modeling framework that introduces hyperbolic emotion-core routing to capture higher-level, abstract emotional representations for MER. The proposed framework jointly models token-level cross-modal semantic interactions and latent emotion representations in two complementary geometric spaces. Specifically, GeoMER consists of a Euclidean Multimodal Fusion Module (EuclFusion) for multimodal representation fusion and a Hyperbolic Geometric Core Routing (HypGeoRoute) module that performs geometry-aware core assignment and aggregation in a hyperbolic manifold to capture more abstract feature representations. The HypGeoRoute module complements the Euclidean token-level modeling. We experiment on the IEMOCAP multimodal MER benchmark and demonstrate that the proposed method achieves the best performance, with 83.54% weighted accuracy and 83.35% unweighted accuracy, outperforming Euclidean baselines, including MemoCMT.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted at ICONIP 2026 (33rd International Conference on Neural Information Processing)</p></details> |
| **[Multimodal Fusion of RGB and Complementary Modalities for Semantic Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17800v1)** | 2026-10-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multi-modal semantic segmentation adds an auxiliary sensing stream to RGB images (RGB-X), such as thermal, LiDAR, depth, event, polarization, or light field. This can improve robustness in poor lighting and under motion blur. But different sensors often produce features that do not match well, and fusion can become unstable. To alleviate these issues, we propose a bidirectional polarity-aware cross-modality fusion (BPCF) module that effectively captures complementary cues while enhancing feature alignment. We evaluate the framework on six modality pairings: RGB-Thermal, RGB-LiDAR, RGB-Depth, RGB-Event, RGB-Polarization, and RGB-Light Field. Our model achieve state-of-the-art (SOTA) results on eight public datasets, including MFNet, KITTI-360, DELIVER, DDD17, DSEC, MCubeS, ZJU, and UrbanLF. The codes and trained models will be released upon acceptance.</p></details> | <details><summary>This ...</summary><p>This work is submitted to the IJCV journal</p></details> |
| **[Focus on What You Need: Global-to-Local Dynamic Attention for Multimodal Sarcasm Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.17707v1)** | 2026-10-13 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal sarcasm detection has attracted increasing attention due to the widespread use of images and text on social media platforms. Existing studies generally treat the semantic or emotional inconsistency between images and text as a key cue for identifying sarcastic expressions. However, images in real-world scenarios often contain multiple visual objects, many of which are irrelevant to sarcasm-related semantics. Such redundant visual information may introduce noise during cross-modal interaction, distracting models from focusing on critical sarcasm cues and potentially leading to inaccurate predictions. To address this issue, we propose GLDA (Global-to-Local Dynamic Attention), a multimodal sarcasm detection framework that models cross-modal interactions from a global-to-local perspective. Specifically, GLDA first employs a global multimodal fusion module to capture high-level semantic interactions between visual and textual modalities, enabling the model to identify coarse-grained cross-modal inconsistencies. To further reduce the influence of irrelevant objects, GLDA introduces a local gated cross-modal attention mechanism that dynamically filters redundant visual and textual features and refines multimodal representations at a finer granularity. In addition, a multi-branch learning objective is designed to jointly optimize unimodal and multimodal representations. Experimental results demonstrate that GLDA effectively improves sarcasm detection performance and achieves competitive results compared with existing approaches. The code will be available at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/GLDA-DBE6.</p></details> | 10 pages, 8 figures |
| **[Silence and Noise: Resolving Suppression and Overvaluation in Multimodal Entity Alignment](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.16106v1)** | 2026-10-12 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal entity alignment (MMEA) is fundamental to integrating heterogeneous knowledge graphs (KGs), yet existing paradigms remain critically vulnerable to incomplete modalities and cross-graph structural asymmetries. This paper exposes a fundamental oversight in current MMEA research: treating feature missingness and topological discrepancies as isolated preprocessing or denoising challenges, which overlooks their destructive coupling during multimodal fusion. We systematically formulate and evaluate two newly identified failure modes: 1) Detail Missing (DM)-induced overvaluation, where the absence of local modality details causes models to overemphasize uninformative, blurred representations; and 2) Context Imbalance (CI)-induced suppression, where topological discrepancies across KGs allow dense structural contexts to overwhelm weaker but highly discriminative modal signals, triggering high-level semantic drift. To break this cycle of degradation, we introduce ALMEA, a generative and calibration framework that establishes a bidirectional feedback loop between low-level feature reconstruction and high-level latent space alignment. Specifically, Cross-Modality Imputation (CMI) synthesizes missing modality details to mitigate overvaluation, while Latent Semantic Calibration (LSC) aligns cross-graph joint embeddings to suppress topology-driven semantic drift. Under sparse supervision, we develop Controlled Iterative Selection (CIS) to adaptively acquire representative and diverse pseudo-labeled pairs, optimizing latent calibration under constrained budgets. Extensive evaluations on standard benchmarks demonstrate that ALMEA consistently outperforms state-of-the-art baselines. At a 20% supervision rate, ALMEA yields substantial absolute improvements of up to 5.20% in MRR and 6.40% in Hits@1, maintaining robust gains across higher supervision thresholds.</p></details> | <details><summary>26 pa...</summary><p>26 pages, including appendices. Code: https://github.com/RTX4090123/ALMEA</p></details> |
| **[Design Choices for Multimodal Transformer Learning with Missing and Longitudinal Healthcare Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.14436v1)** | 2026-10-10 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal learning offers the potential to improve predictions by integrating complementary information from heterogeneous data sources. However, multimodal datasets are frequently incomplete, raising important questions about how models should handle missing information. Although recent approaches increasingly rely on Transformer-based architectures for multimodal fusion, incomplete multimodal data introduces challenges such as missing modalities and intra-modal missingness, which motivate key architectural design choices, such as tokenization granularity. In addition, many datasets are longitudinal, with observations collected across multiple time points. The sequential nature of the Transformer architectures provides a natural framework for modeling such data, but introduces additional considerations, such as how temporal information should be represented. In this study, we systematically investigate these design choices using three different cancer datasets. We compare modality-level and feature-level tokenization and evaluate attention masking and imputation as strategies for handling missing information, both naturally occurring in the datasets and synthetically induced. We further investigate approaches for incorporating temporal information in our training process and how to model it. Experiments are conducted with unimodal and multimodal configurations, as well as different levels of missingness for binary survival classification and multiple time encoding strategies for discrete-time survival prediction. Our results show that while multimodal outperforms unimodal results, missing data heavily impacts predictions and thus effective design choices such as tokenization granularity, and intra-missing handling is essential for reliable prediction.</p></details> |  |
| **[Circumpolar Wall-to-Wall Detection of Retrogressive Thaw Slumps with Scarce Labels and Extreme Class Imbalance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.00046v1)** | 2026-10-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Retrogressive thaw slumps (RTS) are among the most dynamic ground deformations caused by permafrost thaw. Abrupt thaw processes such as RTS could roughly double the contribution of permafrost thaw to climate warming, and already threaten a third of Arctic infrastructure. Circumpolar RTS mapping has advanced quickly, yet released inventories together cover only part of the permafrost region. RTS occupy only 0.1-0.5% of image tiles and labels are costly, so a detector must be designed and evaluated as a rare-target problem. We therefore developed a deep learning system and mapped 20.68 M square kilometres of circumpolar permafrost using 41.6 M tiles of 4.8 m PlanetScope imagery. Training used 22,259 tiles with 2,609 RTS instances in 1,718 of them, and the model was tuned through 172 controlled experiments. A convolutional network matched every alternative tested, including multimodal fusion, foundation-model encoders and masked-autoencoder pretraining, each at far more compute for similar or worse results. Labelled data moved performance most, and a foundation model's advantage appeared only when labels were scarce. Tested once on held-out ecoregions at the real-world prevalence of 0.1-0.5%, the final model achieved an area under the precision-recall curve of 0.812-0.855, an object precision of 0.854 and F1 of 0.644. Deployed wall-to-wall, the model produced a circumpolar map of 19,068 high-confidence detections whose sampled precision ranges from 0.54 to 0.90 by size class. We present an evaluation protocol for rare targets, a controlled account of what governs accuracy under scarce labels, and the circumpolar RTS map the validated model produced.</p></details> |  |
| **[Multimodal Fusion for Fine-Grained Classification of Breast Fibroadenoma and Phyllodes Tumors](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.02091v2)** | 2026-10-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Breast fibroadenoma (FA) and phyllodes tumor (PT) are fibroepithelial breast lesions with highly overlapping appearances on B-mode ultrasound, making benign and borderline PT prone to being misclassified as FA and complicating preoperative decision-making. Based on this dataset, we propose a clinically guided multimodal framework that integrates DenseNet-based visual encoding, CLIP-inspired text encoding, and lightweight clinical encoding, and further introduces clinical-conditioned adaptive modulation, cross-modal Transformer fusion, and dual-path representation learning to improve feature alignment and multimodal interaction. Under patient-level five-fold cross-validation, the proposed method achieves an accuracy of 77.64%, F1-score of 73.38%, and AUC of 89.74%, outperforming representative CNN-, Transformer-, and vision-language-based baselines. Ablation studies and class-balanced evaluations further confirm the contribution of three-modality fusion and the key architectural components. Overall, this work provides an effective multimodal approach for fine-grained FA-PT classification and establishes a high-quality benchmark for multimodal breast ultrasound analysis.</p></details> |  |
| **[ITO: Multi-View Alignment and Training-Time Fusion for Image-Text Pretraining](https://arxiv.org/abs/2603.02767v5)** | 2026-10-09 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Image--text contrastive pretraining has become a dominant paradigm for visual representation learning, yet existing methods often yield representations that remain partially organized by modality rather than by semantics. We propose ITO, a framework addressing this limitation through two complementary mechanisms with distinct roles. Multimodal multiple alignment enriches supervision by constructing diverse cross-modal correspondences from multi-view image augmentations, providing the primary source of discriminative gain. A lightweight training-time multimodal fusion module then acts as a training-time regularizer, encouraging the encoders to produce features that are compatible under fusion. Crucially, the fusion module is discarded at inference, preserving the efficiency of standard dual-encoder architectures while incurring training-time overhead only. Extensive experiments across pretraining scales from millions to billions of image--text pairs show that ITO outperforms strong contrastive baselines at moderate scale and yields consistent gains over CLIP under identical data, backbone, and compute at the 100M--1B scale, on classification, retrieval, and multimodal benchmarks. Controlled comparisons show that these gains are not explained by additional training compute or image augmentation alone. Our analysis further reveals that the contribution of fusion grows with data scale and that it stabilizes optimization, mitigating the late-stage overfitting commonly observed in aggressive contrastive learning. Code is available at https://github.com/showstarpro/ITO.</p></details> |  |
| **[Evi-VN: Hard Region Guided Virtual Node Evidence Injection for GNN-Based Fraud Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11665v1)** | 2026-10-08 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Online platforms contain growing numbers of bots, deceptive reviewers, and scam accounts that imitate legitimate users. Such camouflage blurs graph neighborhoods and behavioral attributes, making it difficult for graph neural networks (GNNs) to distinguish both well-disguised fraudsters and legitimate users. Across diverse GNNs, we observe overlapping errors on a shared hard region, suggesting the presence of latent fraud evidence that graph topologies and standard features fail to capture. Fraud-specific GNNs can mitigate particular graph pathologies, yet they still make limited use of heterogeneous evidence such as structured records, text, images, and audio; uniform multimodal fusion may also disturb nodes already handled reliably by the graph. We propose Evi-VN to learn and correct these shared blind spots rather than build another fraud detector. To our knowledge, Evi-VN is the first graph fraud detection framework to use feature isolated evidence chains to correct hard regions shared across GNNs. Its evidence chains connect behavior, content, and context across structured, textual, visual, and acoustic sources, helping expose camouflage that graph neighborhoods may miss. Crucially, Evi-VN selectively applies this evidence only to likely hard samples via virtual class nodes, preserving both the reliable predictions and the input design of existing GNNs. Shared hard regions also let Evi-VN enhance generic, fraud-specific, and unseen GNNs even with imperfect evidence models. Experiments across bot, fake-review, refund-evidence, and telecom-fraud tasks validate these advantages.</p></details> | <details><summary>17 pa...</summary><p>17 pages, including supplementary material</p></details> |
| **[CoPoE: Multimodal Fusion via Decomposable Disease-Coordinate Product-of-Experts for Missing-Modality Alzheimer's Diagnosis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.11394v1)** | 2026-10-08 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal Alzheimer's disease (AD) diagnosis benefits from integrating heterogeneous clinical, imaging, genomic, and biomarker evidence, but clinical cohorts frequently suffer from irregular modality missingness. Existing fusion methods often synthesize absent inputs, risking the introduction of artificial surrogates, or pool available signals into uninterpretable latent spaces. We present CoPoE (Disease-Coordinate Product-of-Experts), a disease-coordinate framework that maps multimodal evidence into a structured latent space partitioned into four distinct biological and clinical axes: genetic Risk, molecular Pathology, Neurodegeneration, and clinical Stage (R/P/N/S). Each observed modality parameterizes a diagonal Gaussian expert over the full RPNS vector, and a masked Product-of-Experts architecture fuses only the available modalities. Consequently, absent modalities add no factor to the fusion path, allowing the network to preserve a robust, decomposable posterior for any non-empty modality subset without synthetic imputation in the RPNS path. Through extensive missing-modality experiments on the ADNI dataset, CoPoE achieves the best all-modality performance and the highest mean AUROC across all 15 observed-subset evaluations among standardized missing-modality fusion baselines under a shared non-PET ADNI embedding benchmark, while substantially improving raw-probability ECE, Brier score, and NLL. Furthermore, PET-supervised probing shows evidence enrichment within the pathology (P) block under full modalities, with tau-related signal retained even when direct fluid biospecimen inputs are withheld. Our code is available at https://github.com/labhai/CoPoE.</p></details> | <details><summary>Accep...</summary><p>Accepted at IEEE BIBM 2026</p></details> |
| **[DynaConTalk: Wavelet-Constrained Diffusion for Long-Form and Controllable Holistic Co-Speech 3D Motion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.09846v1)** | 2026-10-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Holistic co-speech animation is prone to averaging in both motion representation and speech conditioning. In coordinate-space diffusion, slow body posture, mid-frequency gesture strokes, and fast hand or facial details are entangled in one prediction target, often producing low-variance, over-smoothed motion. Meanwhile, dense rhythmic and acoustic cues can dominate sparse content-specific information under fixed multimodal fusion. We present DynaConTalk, a wavelet-constrained diffusion framework for long-form and controllable holistic co-speech motion generation. Diffusion operates in stationary wavelet transform (SWT) coefficient space, whose temporally aligned bands separate coarse posture evolution, gesture strokes, and fine expressive details. Our dynamic gating network preserves HuBERT and speaker identity as a base and selectively adds rhythm, mel, and transcript features through motion-state- and noise-aware residual gates. Attention pooling and learned depth routing deliver complementary conditions to each denoising stage, while a frame-resolution rhythm path preserves precise timing. A signed proposal-consensus update then reconciles these conditions with the evolving motion state. Matched-noise constraint injection uses the same sampling interface for history continuation and localized keypose repair, and extends to reference-guided control. Separate body-hand and facial denoisers, followed by inverse SWT and a pose-driven root regressor, produce holistic motion. Experiments evaluate generation quality, facial accuracy, temporal continuity, and controllable editing. Code, models, and the interactive editing interface are available at https://github.com/zhuyifeiabcd1/DynaConTalk.</p></details> | <details><summary>14 pa...</summary><p>14 pages, 11 figures. Code and models: https://github.com/zhuyifeiabcd1/DynaConTalk</p></details> |
| **[Batch Augmentation with Unimodal Fine-tuning for Multimodal Fusion of Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.06592v2)** | 2026-10-07 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>In this paper, we propose batch augmentation with unimodal fine-tuning for multimodal learning. We start with pre-trained unimodal models. We fine-tune the unimodal models with the application data. After that, we form a Multi-Layer Perceptron (MLP) head that takes information from unimodal models and provides output. Finally, we train the MLP layer and unimodal parts with batch augmentation. Depending on the data, some unimodal models can be replaced by hard-coded scripts or AI agents. The unimodal training can also follow batch augmentation when the data is augmentable. We write a multimodal batch augmentation dataloader script that implements the batch augmentation for the multimodal data. We investigate the proposed method on the FPU23 ultrasound and UPMC Food-101 multimodal datasets. The multimodal large language model (LLM) with the proposed training achieves the best average result among the investigated methods across both datasets. According to our literature search, the proposed method achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) accuracy of 93.29% on the UPMC Food-101 dataset, while we apply the ViT-L/16 model for vision and the GPT-2 model for text. We share the scripts of the proposed method with traditional counterparts at the following repository: github.com/dipuk0506/multimodal</p></details> |  |
| **[Improving Fake Profile Detection Through Multimodal Learning and Research-Guided Feature Integration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23926v1)** | 2026-10-06 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Fake accounts continue to undermine online platforms, yet it remains unclear whether multimodal learning offers meaningful improvements over strong metadata baselines or graph-based approaches. | **[Quantum Entangled Multimodal Fusion Networks (QEMFN): Resource-Aware Hybrid Vision-Language Fusion via Trainable Entanglement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.08216v1)** | 2026-10-06 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal vision-language systems typically fuse image and text embeddings through classical operators such as concatenation, attention, bilinear pooling, or tensor interactions. We propose Quantum Entangled Multimodal Fusion Networks (QEMFN), a hybrid quantum-classical framework that introduces parameterized entanglement as a structured inductive bias for multimodal fusion. Pretrained visual and textual features are projected into compact latent spaces, encoded as angle-parameterized quantum states, processed through intra-modal and paired cross-modal entangling circuits, and measured to produce fused representations for retrieval. Under matched parameter budgets and identical frozen CLIP backbones, QEMFN outperforms classical fusion baselines on COCO-5k and Flickr30k, including multilayer perceptron, tensor fusion, FiLM, cross-attention, compact transformer, and a dequantized paired-topology analogue. An ablation suite isolates the quantum module's contribution from the surrounding classical projections, and quantum-centric analyses report Meyer-Wallach entangling capability, expressibility, gradient variance against barren-plateau bounds, and entropy-performance correlation under controls for training progress alongside an intervention study on the entangling component. QEMFN is executed under shot-based estimation, a noise-modeled fake backend, and a real superconducting device with zero-noise extrapolation. This work does not claim quantum computational advantage; the contribution is the framework together with a controlled empirical and quantum-centric evaluation that positions trainable entanglement as an interpretable, hardware-executable fusion mechanism at scales accessible on contemporary devices.</p></details> |  |
| **[CHOMP: Multimodal Chewing Side Detection with Earphones](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.02233v2)** | 2026-10-06 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Chewing-side preference (CSP) is a risk factor for temporomandibular disorders (TMDs) and a behavioral manifestation. Although TMDs affect roughly one-third of the global population, assessment relies on clinical examinations and self-reports, providing limited insight into everyday jaw function. We present CHOMP, the first earphone-based chewing-side detection system for continuous CSP monitoring. Using OpenEarable 2.0, we collected multimodal data from 20 participants with microphones, a bone-conduction microphone, IMU, PPG, and a pressure sensor across diverse foods, activities, and acoustic-interference conditions. Multimodal fusion reaches 98.0% under LOFO and 97.3% under adapted LOSO. We demonstrate CHOMP's performance under three acoustic-interference conditions and within a cafeteria setting. Our results establish earphones as a practical platform for everyday CSP monitoring and jaw-function assessment.</p></details> |  |
| **[Multimodal Knowledge Distillation for Gastric Adenocarcinoma Classification from Whole-Slide Images](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.07913v1)** | 2026-10-06 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Gastric adenocarcinoma (GA) is a leading cause of cancer-related mortality worldwide, and accurate histopathological subtype classification from whole-slide images (WSIs) is essential for effective treatment planning. Each WSI is represented as a bag of patches paired with a slide-level diagnostic caption. The teacher model learns fused image-text representations for subtype classification, while the student model distills this knowledge to enable accurate image-only inference. We evaluate our method on the PatchGastric benchmark dataset and achieve at least 3.35% higher mean accuracy than state-of-the-art approaches, without relying on transformer-based fusion, multi-task learning, or large language models. The source code is available at https://github.com/helomelo1/MKD-LMF.</p></details> |  |
| **[Anatomy-aware Fine-grained Multimodal Fusion for Laryngopharyngeal Cancer T-Staging Prediction Using CT and Radiology Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.06837v1)** | 2026-10-05 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Accurate T-staging is crucial for guiding personalized treatment strategies for laryngopharyngeal cancer. However, current clinical practice relies on invasive biopsy procedures, whereas CT-based staging remains challenging due to the complex patterns of tumor invasion. The framework first constructs an Anatomy-Structured Organ Graph (AOG) that captures invasion patterns between primary sites and surrounding organs, then performs Organ-Anchored Cross-Modal Alignment (OCA) so that each organ node aggregates textual evidence from the radiology report, and finally refines this graph representation by injecting organ-specific invasion cues extracted from the report via Report-Enhanced Graph-Refinement (REG), yielding a multimodal organ graph that combines spatial and textual evidence. | **[FedMHAR: Federated Multimodal Human Activity Recognition using Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.33492v2)** | 2026-10-03 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Human Activity Recognition (HAR) from heterogeneous wearable sensors is fundamental to the Internet of Health Things (IoHT), supporting rehabilitation, elderly care, and smart healthcare. Existing multimodal fusion methods often assign fixed equal weights to sensor streams, overlooking differences in modality importance, acquisition cost, and sensor quality, which can vary due to movement, incorrect placement, or temporary blockage. We propose an adaptive and cost-aware multimodal HAR framework based on multi-agent reinforcement learning for centralized HAR and extend it to federated learning as FedMHAR. In the centralized setting, multimodal fusion is formulated as a cooperative Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning (MARL) problem, where each sensing modality is assigned a PPO-based agent that learns per-sample fusion weights, enabling the model to emphasize informative modalities while down-weighting costly sensors when cheaper alternatives provide sufficient information. In the federated setting, we introduce BiFL-PPO, a bidirectional federated optimization strategy in which a server-side PPO policy learns client-specific trust weights and feeds them back to adapt local learning rates and proximal regularization. Unlike round-level optimization, BiFL-PPO uses dense batch-level rewards for more frequent feedback and stable training under heterogeneous client data. Evaluation on the MEx Rehabilitation and UTD Multimodal Human Action datasets shows that the centralized framework achieves 87.30% and 94.98% accuracy, respectively, outperforming conventional fusion methods and state-of-the-art HAR models. | **[Architecture-Dependent Fusion Pathways in MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.03289v1)** | 2026-10-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) achieve strong performance across vision-language tasks, yet the internal mechanisms by which visual and textual information are fused across layers remain insufficiently understood. Separately, we perform causal intervention experiments as a validation of the resulting interpretation. As a supplementary analysis, we use visual CKA to examine the Platonic Representation Hypothesis. Together, these analyses reveal two distinct fusion pathways: concatenation models follow a text-first, vision-later pathway, whereas native models exhibit earlier visual-textual co-adaptation and feature-space reorganization. This work provides a mechanistic perspective for understanding multimodal fusion and supports architecture-aware diagnostics of multimodal representations.</p></details> |  |
| **[Multimodal Ambivalence/Hesitancy Recognition in Videos for Personalized Digital Health Interventions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2604.11730v5)** | 2026-10-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Using behavioural science, health interventions focus on behaviour change by providing a framework to help patients acquire and maintain healthy habits that improve medical outcomes. In-person interventions are costly and difficult to scale, especially in resource-limited regions. Digital health interventions offer a cost-effective approach, potentially supporting independent living and self-management. Automating such interventions, especially through machine learning, has recently gained considerable attention. Ambivalence and hesitancy (A/H) play a primary role for individuals to delay, avoid, or abandon health interventions. A/H are subtle and conflicting emotions that place a person in a state between positive and negative evaluations of a behaviour, or between acceptance and refusal to engage in it. They manifest as affective inconsistency across modalities or within a modality, such as language, facial, vocal expressions, and body language. While experts can be trained to recognize A/H, integrating them into digital health interventions is costly and less effective. Automatic A/H recognition is therefore critical for the personalization and cost-effectiveness of digital health interventions. Here, we explore the application of deep learning models for A/H recognition in videos, a multi-modal task by nature. In particular, this paper covers three learning setups: supervised learning, unsupervised domain adaptation for personalization, and zero-shot inference via large language models (LLMs). Our experiments are conducted on the unique and recently published BAH video dataset for A/H recognition. Our results show limited performance, suggesting that more adapted multi-modal models are required for accurate A/H recognition. Better methods for modeling spatio-temporal and multimodal fusion are necessary to leverage conflicts within/across modalities.</p></details> | <details><summary>11 pa...</summary><p>11 pages, 4 figures, ACII 2026. arXiv admin note: substantial text overlap with arXiv:2505.19328</p></details> |
| **[GAANet: Global-guided Asymmetric Attention Network for Audio-Visual Speech Separation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.02752v1)** | 2026-10-02 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Multi-scale design is crucial for efficient audio-visual speech separation, yet effectively modeling multi-scale information for audio-visual feature fusion remains challenging. We argue that the limited capacity of existing approaches primarily arises from: 1) treating features from different modalities in the same manner, and 2) overlooking the role of global features. To address these issues, we propose a Global-guided Asymmetric Attention Network (GAANet). Our model introduces two core innovations: first, an asymmetric multi-scale fusion framework that allows audio and visual streams to extract and interact with features at their respective optimal temporal resolutions, removing the need for symmetric temporal downsampling; second, a global-guided attention mechanism that compresses each modality into a compact global token with a temporal dimension of one, which then provides high-level semantic cues to guide both intra- and inter-modal fusion across scales. Experiments on LRS2 and VoxCeleb2 demonstrate that GAANet achieves state-of-the-art performance, reaching 16.5 dB SI-SNRi on LRS2 and 14.0 dB on VoxCeleb2, while maintaining a lightweight computational profile with only 3.3M parameters and 19.8G MACs. These results highlight the strong potential of asymmetric temporal modeling and global guidance for efficient and robust multimodal fusion. | **[HADRec: A Hierarchy-Aware Drug Recommendation Framework by Fusing Molecular Knowledge and Electronic Health Record](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.00984v1)** | 2026-10-01 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Accurate medication recommendation is central to clinical decision-making, directly determining therapeutic efficacy and patient safety. Extensive experiments on state-of-the-art MLLMs reveal that visual-only inputs remain challenging, structural chemistry is the hardest domain, and multimodal fusion mitigates but does not eliminate visual, knowledge-based, or logical errors, highlighting ChemVTS-Bench as a rigorous, domain-faithful testbed for advancing multimodal chemical reasoning. | **[Context-Aware RGB-Thermal-Radar Obstacle Perception for Resource-Constrained ASVs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23873v1)** | 2026-09-19 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Reliable obstacle perception is challenging for small autonomous surface vessels (ASVs): reflections and glare confound visible-light cameras, vessel motion shifts the apparent waterline, and darkness removes the dominant sensing modality altogether. It also distinguishes sensor dropout from a valid observation with no detected obstacle. Using new multimodal lake recordings, we deployed the pipeline in a 24-leg closed-loop pilot under uncontrolled lake conditions (16 day, 4 dusk, and 4 night; 351 m), including night legs in which RGB was unusable and the vessel drove on LWIR and radar evidence alone. On eight held-out low-light recordings, the calibrated fusion model achieved average precision (AP) 0.869 and expected calibration error 0.108, compared with AP 0.673 for rule-based fusion, 0.620 for gated fusion, and 0.903 for RGB alone. Our results expose a central tradeoff: RGB ranks best when usable, whereas fusion preserves a navigation input when RGB fails. To further enhance aspect-oriented reasoning capabilities, we propose a dependency-syntax-guided sentiment cue strategy. | **[QoS-Aware Federated Learning for Multimodal In-Cabin Interaction in Smart Vehicles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2609.20123v1)** | 2026-09-17 | <details><summary>Show</summary><p>Modern smart vehicles leverage multimodal sensors, ranging from high-bandwidth vision systems to low-rate physiological monitors, to provide personalized in-cabin services. In this paper, we propose FedQoS, a novel asynchronous, event-triggered FL framework that decouples local computation from global communication via a two-phase gating mechanism. First, we introduce a resource-aware training gate that initializes local learning only when sensing buffers and energy reserves meet safety thresholds, preventing ML tasks from compromising core vehicle mobility. Second, a QoS-aware transmission policy gates uplink updates based on an efficiency score that balances model novelty against instantaneous latency and energy costs. Extensive experiments on multimodal vehicular datasets demonstrate that FedQoS achieves competitive personalized accuracy with only marginal performance loss compared to FedAvg, while substantially reducing QoS violations, cutting communication overhead by 76.7\%, and lowering latency cost by 26.0\%, demonstrating a highly favorable accuracy and efficiency balance for real-world vehicular deployments.</p></details> |  |

## Information Fusion
